 succeeded in 0ms:
---
name: code-review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes — Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** — does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** — does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you — run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point — a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here — not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) — fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

On top of whatever the repo documents, the Standards axis always carries the **smell baseline** below — a fixed set of Fowler code smells (_Refactoring_, ch.3) that applies even when a repo documents nothing. Two rules bind it:

- **The repo overrides.** A documented repo standard always wins; where it endorses something the baseline would flag, suppress the smell.
- **Always a judgement call.** Each smell is a labelled heuristic ("possible Feature Envy"), never a hard violation — and, like any standard here, skip anything tooling already enforces.

Each smell reads *what it is* → *how to fix*; match it against the diff:

- **Mysterious Name** — a function, variable, or type whose name doesn't reveal what it does or holds. → rename it; if no honest name comes, the design's murky.
- **Duplicated Code** — the same logic shape appears in more than one hunk or file in the change. → extract the shared shape, call it from both.
- **Feature Envy** — a method that reaches into another object's data more than its own. → move the method onto the data it envies.
- **Data Clumps** — the same few fields or params keep travelling together (a type wanting to be born). → bundle them into one type, pass that.
- **Primitive Obsession** — a primitive or string standing in for a domain concept that deserves its own type. → give the concept its own small type.
- **Repeated Switches** — the same `switch`/`if`-cascade on the same type recurs across the change. → replace with polymorphism, or one map both sites share.
- **Shotgun Surgery** — one logical change forces scattered edits across many files in the diff. → gather what changes together into one module.
- **Divergent Change** — one file or module is edited for several unrelated reasons. → split so each module changes for one reason.
- **Speculative Generality** — abstraction, parameters, or hooks added for needs the spec doesn't have. → delete it; inline back until a real need shows.
- **Message Chains** — long `a.b().c().d()` navigation the caller shouldn't depend on. → hide the walk behind one method on the first object.
- **Middle Man** — a class or function that mostly just delegates onward. → cut it, call the real target direct.
- **Refused Bequest** — a subclass or implementer that ignores or overrides most of what it inherits. → drop the inheritance, use composition.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** — include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3, **plus the smell baseline from step 3** pasted in full — the sub-agent has no other access to it.
- The brief: "Report — per file/hunk where relevant — (a) every place the diff violates a documented standard: cite the standard (file + the rule); and (b) any baseline smell you spot: name it and quote the hunk. Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls — documented-standard breaches can be hard, but baseline smells are always judgement calls, and a documented repo standard overrides the baseline. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** — include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings — the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes — that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing → **Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions → **Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

+    <section class="rung2" id="rung2" aria-labelledby="r2-heading">
+      <p class="eyebrow">Second rung: free-energy-matched</p>
+      <h2 id="r2-heading">Same question, with fairer comparators and a better-powered readout</h2>
+      <p>The first rung could not tell whether a negative came from the field or from the receiver-alone controls holding more free energy (for one block, 22,169 against 9,917, at equal total energy). This rung adds comparators that match the field's free energy exactly, sums each arm over several replicate runs to push the noise floor down, and asks whether a hand-built polymer wall of a realistic dose can be seen at all before any fresh builder block is run.</p>
+      <p class="disclosure">Exploratory, CPU only, no GPU evidence, mutation off. Local, kept off the public site. A negative would only mean a consistent benefit was not detected under these rules, not that there is no benefit and not that a second link is impossible.</p>

   "note": "not replayed; excluded from the deterministic payload",
     1	# I06b Free-energy-matched comparators: niche construction, second rung (slice plan v2, after Codex plan review)
     2	
     3	Status: PLAN stage, 2026-11-02. Branch `claude/i06-free-energy-matched` from `origin/main` 0bdd7a70. Sources: "I06" in `docs/simulation-research-2026-11-01/report.md`; the first rung `docs/niche-construction-2026-11-01/` (PLAN, EVIDENCE, REVIEW, sealed `evidence.json`); coordinator decisions D1, D3 in `/Users/nicholas/develop/browser-life-run-1102-log/docs/backlog-run-2026-11-02.html`. Exploratory, CPU only (`sim-ref`; GPU is allowed by the scope but not needed), mutation off, no physics change, no model calls, no deploy. v2 revisions answer `REVIEW.md` "Plan review dispositions" (P1-1..5, P2-6..14). This is a rung on the same tool, not a fork: every new behaviour is keyed off a second protocol object so the first rung's sealed evidence still verifies byte for byte.
     4	
     5	## 1. Bounded question
     6	
     7	Rung 1 ended `unsupported-measurement` for all three builder candidates (clusters 0, 4, 9). Three defects, each named in `EVIDENCE.md`:
     8	
     9	1. **Comparator confound.** Receiver-alone controls (d0, d1E, d1B) match *total* energy but not *free* energy E (cluster 0, seed 8600501: 9,917 vs 22,169, both totals 26,969). The negative inoculum contrasts (23 of 24 blocks) therefore cannot be blamed on inoculum alone.
    10	2. **Noise floor above the effect.** Dev replicate floors delta = 341 / 291 / 404 against builder polymer of 103.5 / 98 / 84.5 and a receiver reserve of B_r = 480.
    11	3. **No positive control at realistic scale.** The hand wall was detected only at 1200 polymer (about 10x what builders leave); at 120 it was not (W1 4/8, W2 3/8 on dev).
    12	
    13	Question for this rung: when the receiver-alone benchmark is matched on free energy as well as on total energy and matter, and the readout is powered to a noise floor below the effect it must detect, (a) does the assay pass a positive control at a stated, realistic polymer dose, and, only if so, (b) does a builder's conditioned field causally raise a receiver's receiver-labelled biomass inventory (survival is a viability requirement on the field arm, not a claimed benefit; a paired survival contrast is reported as attribution only) relative to nonbuilder, ablation, scramble and the free-energy-matched receiver-alone controls?
    14	
    15	Valid outcomes, all reported as such: `supported` for a pair; `unsupported-within-tested-domain` (a usable negative, only when the positive control passed); `unsupported-measurement` with a stated minimum detectable polymer dose (the "stated reason none exists" branch of the scope); `invalid-or-incomplete`. A negative here does not say a second link is impossible, does not test mutation reaching builder or receiver, and does not test A+B->C.
    16	
    17	## 2. What is built (one PR)
    18	
    19	1. **Evidence fixtures with a negative case that fools the old inoculum contrast** (section 4.5). New fixture `far-field-energy`: a conditioned field that differs from its controls only by extra free energy laid in a band outside the inoculum footprint plus halo (no polymer). Because ablation only converts P to C (`transfer.ts`), with no polymer `b` and `ab` stay identical and `D_poly = 0`, so the complete rung-1 gate and the new gate both reject it. What the fixture tests is the **inoculum contrast alone**: rung-1 `D_inoc` (vs d0/d1E/d1B/region-matched) reads positive (fooled) while `D_inoc2` (with fU/fS/fB) does not. Evidence records `fullGateLabel` (unsupported, by `D_poly`) and `inocFooledOld` / `inocCaughtNew` separately; the claim made is about the contrast, never "the old rule would label it supported". A full-gate trap needs a polymer-bearing confound and is deferred (section 11). If the fixture cannot fool `D_inoc` at unit scale, the test fails and the slice reports that instead of weakening the claim.
    20	2. **Small CPU path, same library.** Extensions in `packages/runner/src/niche/`: second protocol (`PROTOCOL_FE`), free-energy-matched comparators fU, fS, fB, R-replicate Stage-2 readout, dose-ladder positive control, decision with `inoc2`. `tools/niche/niche.ts` gains `--rung fe` (selects the second protocol and docs directory) in front of every existing mode; no new CLI.
    21	3. **Manifest and machine-readable evidence:** `docs/niche-construction-fe-2026-11-02/{evidence.json,manifest.json,calibration.json,protocol.lock.json}`, sealed like rung 1 with `--write`, `--check`, `--replay-check`.
    22	4. **Local page.** `apps/lab/niche/index.html` gains a "Second rung: free-energy-matched" section (same private route, still 404 on the deployed site) reading the second `evidence.json`: free-energy table per comparator (the 9,917 vs 22,169 style numbers, before and after matching), noise floor vs effect bar, dose-ladder positive-control chart, edge per candidate, the `far-field-energy` panel. Reads evidence only, runs no simulation.
    23	5. Docs: this PLAN (carries the dated amendment, section 10), `REVIEW.md` (Codex dispositions), `EVIDENCE.md` (human result, written after the run).
    24	
    25	## 3. Reuse and honest limits of reuse
    26	
    27	- Reuse as is: `transfer.ts` (ledger, relabel, placement, scramble), `arms.ts` builders, `assay.ts` stage runner and readouts, `decision.ts` sign test / intersection-union / Holm, `evidence.ts` sealing, the rung-1 page helpers (`render.ts`), `EVIDENCE_STATUSES`. Rung-1 `PROTOCOL` object and every rung-1 code path stay byte-identical in behaviour; `--check` and `--replay-check` for rung 1 are acceptance gates (G7).
    28	- **Registry binding.** Rung 1 hashes the whole registry (minus owners listed in `LATER_SLICE_OWNERS`). This rung adds the owner prefix `i06b free-energy-matched` to that list (the I08 precedent) and binds its own digest over its own reservation only, so neither rung's evidence is invalidated by the other's reservation.
    29	- **Audit.** `transfer.ts` `Audit`/`residuals` have no credit term and `runArm` audits every census. This rung adds **no credit to the audit**: each arm's ledger baseline is that arm's own constructed starting total (lattice + reserve, heatAnalytic 0), so each arm's physical residual is exactly `0n` as in rung 1. The construction difference between `b` and a comparator is a comparison-budget `constructionGap` recorded in evidence only, not a ledger entry (it is not energy created inside any run). If the existing audit cannot take a per-arm baseline, add an optional `baseline` parameter defaulting to current behaviour and prove rung-1 serialization unchanged by test.
    30	- **Manifest conflict (G7).** `tools/niche/niche.ts` `sourceFiles` hashes every niche `.ts` and `checkSaved` rejects changed or added files, so editing existing niche sources or adding modules fails rung-1 `--check` unless the rung-1 `manifest.json` is re-pinned. As in the I08 precedent (rung-1 REVIEW dated note 2026-11-02), a **manifest-only re-pin** of `docs/niche-construction-2026-11-01/manifest.json` is allowed; `evidence.json`, `calibration.json`, `protocol.lock.json`, PLAN, EVIDENCE and REVIEW of rung 1 stay byte-identical to `origin/main`, and rung-1 `--check` and `--replay-check` must pass after the re-pin. New rung-fe code goes in new files wherever possible to keep the diff to existing sources small.
    31	- No change to `packages/schema`, `packages/sim-ref`, `packages/sim-gpu`, `defaultConfig()`, `RULE_VERSION`, golden pins, vendor, preregistration.
    32	- All new arithmetic is analytical, integer (BigInt where it touches totals), outside the step function.
    33	
    34	## 4. Design
    35	
    36	Everything in rung-1 PLAN section 4 holds unless stated: single 24x24 tile, kernelRadius 5, mutation off, ladder genes, recurring injury with shell, T1 = 60, T2 = 80, reserve R = (480, 960), footprint (14,12) r3, halo 2, absolute clock T1 for every Stage-2 arm, candidates by panel `{0, 4, 9}` and receiver cluster 12, drop order 9 then 4 by runtime only.
    37	
    38	### 4.1 Free-energy-matched comparators (answers defect 1)
    39	
    40	Free energy means lattice channel E (`sum E`), reported next to total energy (`totalsOf`), for the builder-removed arm `b` and each comparator, at the start of Stage 2 (before inoculum placement) and after placement. Matter is identical in every arm (lattice plus reserve), as in rung 1. Observed rung-1 direction (seed 8600501, cluster 0): the receiver-alone control d0 held MORE free E than `b` (22,169 vs 9,917) with equal total (26,969), because `b` holds its energy as bound matter.
    41	
    42	Matching free energy and total energy simultaneously is impossible at equal matter when the comparator has no conditioned composition (bound matter carries energy at eB, eP, eC, eA). Each comparator is a separately constructed initial state (not a transformation of `b` inside a run); its own ledger starts from its own total and conserves exactly. The difference is reported as `constructionGap = totalEnergy(b) - totalEnergy(f)` (signed BigInt, evidence only, never in the ledger): **gap > 0** means the comparator holds less total energy than the field arm; **gap < 0** means it holds more (a stronger comparator, the conservative side for the claim).
    43	
    44	- **fU (uniform free-matched):** receiver-alone lattice, uniform A, free E set uniform to `sum E(b)` over the lattice.
    45	- **fS (spatial free-matched):** as fU but E copied cell for cell from `b` (same free-energy field, no polymer, no biomass, matter all A in the remainder). Isolates "the energy pattern alone" from "structure".
    46	- **fB (free-matched plus biomass-matched):** fS plus the rung-1 d1B raise of B (region plus halo, matter converted from A), **without** the `(eB - eA)*dB` lattice debit, so free E stays equal to `b` cell for cell before and after placement; fB holds `(eB - eA) * dB` more total energy than fS (the bound-energy increase is extra total energy, never a ledger entry). Its own `constructionGap` to `b` is recorded with its sign (positive when the field binds more energy in polymer or waste than fB is handed, negative or zero otherwise; `comparator-excess` where it is negative). Test: free-E equality before and after placement, and the fS-to-fB gap difference.
    47	- Rung-1 d0, d1E, d1B are kept unchanged and reported (total-matched family).
    48	
    49	New inoculum contrast: `D_inoc2 = Y(b | builder) - max(Y(d1E), Y(d1B), Y(fU), Y(fS), Y(fB))`; every constructed comparator is in the max (none excluded by sign of gap; `comparator-excess` and `comparator-deficit` are labels in evidence). Rung-1 `D_inoc` is still computed and reported next to it. The four gating contrasts are builder, poly, space, inoc2. A comparator whose construction would need negative lattice E or exceed `POOL_MAX`/`MATTER_MAX` is refused (`credit-overdraw`, never clamped); a block where fU, fS and fB are all refused is `invalid-or-incomplete` for that candidate (reported, never dropped).
    50	
    51	### 4.2 Better-powered readout (answers defect 2)
    52	
    53	- **Replicated Stage 2.** Each arm's Stage 2 is run for `R = 4` sim seeds (block seed plus fixed offsets `{0, 1, 2, 3} * 1000`), identical offsets in every arm (so injury steps and RNG keys still coincide across arms); Stage 1 histories and transfers are shared. Primary endpoint `Y = sum over the R replicates of receiver-labelled B at T2` (integer; no division, units are x R of rung 1). Viability: receiver B above zero in at least `R - 1` of R replicates counts the field arm `b` as viable for that block. Viability is a requirement for the gate (field arm viable in at least 14 of 16 blocks; at a true per-block rate of 0.9, 15/16 would pass only 51% of the time). A paired survival contrast (arm vs comparator) is reported as attribution, never gating, and no survival benefit is claimed.
    54	- **Replicate seeds** are enumerated and reserved explicitly (5.1) for the maximum R of 8.
    55	- **More blocks:** 16 fresh blocks per candidate (was 8). Exact sign test over 16; `k = 16` gives 1/65,536, `k = 14` gives 137/65,536, `k = 12` gives 2,517/65,536 (hand-enumerated in tests). Candidate threshold by Holm at alpha 0.05 over 3 candidates as before (1/60, 1/40, 1/20 for the three ordered p-values).
    56	- **Power statement (frozen wording, corrected at freeze after the Codex freeze review; see section 10).** With three candidates running the first Holm rejection needs k >= 13 of 16 (p = 697/65,536; 12 if only one candidate runs). A contrast whose true per-block positive probability is 0.75 passes that single threshold 40.5% of the time, 0.85 gives 79%, 0.90 gives 93%, 0.95 gives 99%; four contrasts, delta and viability can only lower the pass rate. A negative is a failure to detect a consistent benefit under these rules, not an exclusion bound on the effect size and not evidence of no benefit. Dev reports the empirical positive-block rate of the wall control at the qualifying dose next to this table.
    57	- **Delta from a replicate control with the same readout:** `delta = max(ceil(0.02 * B_r * R), 1.5 * max|D|)` over dev replicate pairs (nonbuilder vs nonbuilder, builder vs builder, on a disjoint replicate seed set of R sims). With the sum over R the expected floor falls by about sqrt(R) relative to rung 1 after rescaling; dev says whether it did.
    58	- Secondary readout (reported, never gating): per-replicate area under the receiver-B census curve (sum over the 8 censuses), as a lower-variance cross-check.
    59	- **Noise gate (G-NOISE, dev only, before any fresh seed), per candidate:** `delta_c` must be at most half of the wall control's median effect on the **limiting gating contrast** (the one with the smallest median effect among the four), in summed-R units, at that candidate's qualifying dose (4.3). Otherwise the rung stops `unsupported-measurement` and reports the achieved floor and the minimum detectable dose; the fresh seeds are not read.
    60	
    61	### 4.3 Positive control at realistic scale (answers defect 3)
    62	
    63	The hand-wall fixtures (W1 ring, W2 floor under the receiver; rung-1 definitions) run the full four-contrast gate on dev wall seeds across a dose ladder of total polymer `P in {60, 120, 240, 600, 1200}`, ascending, stopping each wall at its first passing dose (that dose is its minimum detectable dose); doses above the ceiling are run only if nothing at or below it passes, to state the minimum detectable dose.
    64	
    65	- **Per-candidate realistic ceiling** `C_c = 2 x` the median dev builder polymer at T1 of candidate c (rung 1 measured 84.5-103.5, so C is about 170-207, which admits 60 and 120, not 240). The control for candidate c uses doses `<= C_c` only.
    66	- **Ordinary placement required.** The rung-1 `wallGateBlock` uses `allowP: true` (overwriting polymer on injured cells), which real transfers (`placeAll` default false) forbid. The qualifying control must place the wall by the ordinary placement rule (no overwrite of existing polymer, same pool-cap and reserve guards) at cells where that is legal; wall variants that need `allowP: true` stay as a reported diagnostic and never qualify the assay. If no ordinary-placement wall geometry exists, the control is `unavailable` and the stated reason is that none exists, ending the rung `unsupported-measurement`.
    67	- **Wall pass rule (dev, 8 wall seeds):** for each of the four gating contrasts, at least 7 of 8 seeds positive (exact p = 9/256) and median >= `delta_c`. A wall passes at dose P iff all four hold. The control **qualifies candidate c** iff W1 or W2 passes at some dose `<= C_c` and G-NOISE holds (4.2).
    68	- A candidate that does not qualify ends `unsupported-measurement` with the minimum detectable dose, its ratio to the builder polymer and the floor that explains it; its fresh blocks are not simulated (a sealed stopped payload is still written, section 8). Candidates qualify independently.
    69	
    70	Dropped from this rung (cut for scope, section 11): the builder founder-biomass dose-response knob.
    71	
    72	### 4.4 Readouts, ledger and flux (unchanged from rung 1, per replicate)
    73	
    74	Per arm and replicate: receiver survival, receiver-labelled biomass inventory, flux attribution (synthesis share; "growth" only at share at least half and no saturated counter), builder cost, before/after inventories. Ledger invariant per arm: lattice + reserve + cumulative heatAnalytic against that arm's own starting total, exact `0n` at every census of both stages, including every replicate (the unchanged rung-1 audit; no credit term). Evidence adds per arm: free E, total energy, `constructionGap` vs `b`.
    75	
    76	### 4.5 Fixtures
    77	
    78	16x16, kernelRadius 5 at unit scale (rung-1 constraint); the positive control is additionally qualified at the frozen scale on dev.
    79	- `far-field-energy` (new, 2.1: fools rung-1 `D_inoc`, not `D_inoc2`; full gate rejects via `D_poly = 0`); also a uniform and an in-footprint variant, `caughtBy` recorded.
    80	- `free-gap-replay`: rung-1 observed direction at fixture scale (d0 holds MORE free E than `b` with equal totals; bound inventories as recorded); asserts fU/fS/fB reproduce `b`'s free E exactly (difference zero), that d0's excess is removed, and that `constructionGap` is recorded with every arm's own ledger residual `0n`.
    81	- `takeover-only`, `null-field`, `hand-wall-gate-W2` (rung-1 fixtures re-run with the second protocol; expectations unchanged).
    82	- Guards re-run: overwrite, pool cap, underfunded reserve, exhausted reserve, plus `credit-overdraw` (a free-matched comparator that would need negative lattice E or exceed a cap is refused, not clamped).
    83	
    84	## 5. Pre-stated rules (frozen before any fresh-seed run; dated amendment in section 10)
    85	
    86	### 5.1 Seeds
    87	Block seeds in `8,601,001-8,601,999` and every derived replicate seed are reserved in `experiments/discovery/seed-registry.json` (owner `i06b free-energy-matched`) **before** anything runs. Replicate sims use block seed + `k * 1000`, `k = 0..7` (R = 4 uses k = 0..3; R = 8 uses k = 0..7), so derived seeds reach 8,608,999: the reservation lists the contiguous range `8,601,001-8,608,999`; the implementer first runs `seedCollisions` over it and, if any existing owner collides, replaces the offset by a collision-free stride and records that in the amendment. The protocol hash binds the explicit enumerated list of every seed that can run (block, replicate, noise, wall, fixture) and the registry digest; `--check` rescans the enumerated list, not only base lists. Never reused: 8,600,101-108, 8,600,501-508, nor any other rung-1 seed. Dev blocks 8,601,001-8,601,008, dev replicate-noise 8,601,201-8,601,204, wall 8,601,301-8,601,308, fixtures 8,601,401-8,601,408, fresh 8,601,501-8,601,516. Dev outcomes may set only `T1, T2` (unchanged unless dev shows cause), `delta_c`, the dose rungs and ceilings, and `R` (4 or 8; one escalation to 8 allowed if the noise gate fails, recorded before the freeze). Fresh seeds are read once, after the freeze commit is pushed and verified (section 9).
    88	
    89	### 5.2 Estimands
    90	Primary `Y` as in 4.2, paired within a block. Gating contrasts: `D_builder`, `D_poly`, `D_space`, `D_inoc2`. Attribution only: `D_spaceP`, `D_spaceNP`, rung-1 `D_inoc`, `D_naive = Y(b) - Y(d0)`.
    91	
    92	### 5.3 Edge label
    93	Rung-1 rules with these changes only: 16 blocks; `inoc2` replaces `inoc`; per-contrast exact one-sided sign test (zeros non-positive) with `median >= delta`; field arm viable in at least 14 of 16 blocks (viability requirement, no survival-benefit claim); candidate p = max over four; Holm across candidates that ran. Adequacy is per candidate: the ordinary-placement positive control qualified that candidate at a dose within its ceiling and G-NOISE held (4.3); the rung-1 polymer criterion is a reported field only. `unsupported-within-tested-domain` only when valid, adequately measured and a gating test fails; its meaning is bounded by the power statement in 4.2. Mechanism text and the three-valued panel verdict as rung 1.
    94

   112	## 7. Tests (named cases)
   113	
   114	`niche-fe-comparators.test.ts`: `fU free E equals b free E exactly and matter equals`; `fS E channel equals b E channel cell for cell`; `fB free E equals b cell for cell before and after placement (no lattice debit, gap < 0 flagged comparator-excess)`; `constructionGap sign: positive when comparator total is lower`; `every arm residual 0n against its own baseline`; `rung-1 audit serialization unchanged`; `credit-overdraw refused not clamped`; `d0 d1E d1B unchanged from rung 1 (state hashes equal)`; `placement set still the intersection across arms`; `every arm starts at absolute step T1 with equal injury steps and RNG keys`.
   115	`niche-fe-readout.test.ts`: `replicate offsets identical in every arm`; `Y is the integer sum of R replicate endpoints`; `R=4 sum noise floor on a no-op control is below R=1 floor scaled by R (dev-scale fixture)`; `instrumented twin equals plain twin (stateHash) per replicate`; `deterministic replay: same block twice equal bytes`; `segmented stage 2 equals continuous`; `survival rule R-1 of R`; `AUC reported not gating`.
   116	`niche-fe-dose.test.ts`: `ceiling derives from dev builder P per candidate`; `no dose above the ceiling can qualify`; `allowP wall never qualifies, ordinary-placement wall required`; `wall pass rule 7 of 8 and median >= delta`; `failure yields minimum detectable dose and a sealed stopped payload`; `dose ladder deterministic across reruns`.
   117	`niche-fe-fixtures.test.ts`: `far-field-energy: naive positive, rung-1 D_inoc positive (fooled), D_inoc2 not positive, full gate unsupported via D_poly = 0 (caughtBy recorded)`; `free-gap-replay closes the free-E gap`; `takeover-only still zero synthesis`; `null-field exact zeros`; `hand-wall-gate-W2 supported`; every guard refuses.
   118	`niche-decision` additions in `niche-fe-readout`: exact sign p for n=16, k=12,14,16 against hand enumeration; Holm resolution at n=16; `no floats in sealed payload`; `panel verdict not negative when positive control failed`; `paired survival contrast reported, not gating`; `power table values (0.75: 40.5%, 0.9: 93.2%)`.
   119	`niche-fe-evidence.test.ts` (artifact tests run only after generation): `evidence.json sealed and verifies`; `manifest hashes match sources`; `--write and --replay-check refuse without a verifying lock and a pushed identical lock (guard test with a stubbed git)`; `fresh seeds and every derived replicate seed disjoint from 8600101-108, 8600501-508 and the live registry`; `stopped payload seals and verifies`; `rung-1 evidence still verifies after the manifest re-pin; rung-1 evidence/calibration/lock bytes equal origin/main`; `--check does not simulate`.
   120	`niche-fe-render.test.ts`: free-energy table rows incl. constructionGap; noise-vs-effect bar; stopped payload (positive control failed) shows "no fresh run" not "negative"; edge supported/unsupported/unavailable; caption "exploratory, CPU only"; `noindex` unchanged.

   122	## 8. Acceptance gates (runnable)
   123	
   124	```bash
   125	export PATH=~/.nvm/versions/node/v24.2.0/bin:$PATH
   126	cd /Users/nicholas/develop/browser-life-i06-free-energy-matched
   127	pnpm install
   128	pnpm typecheck
   129	pnpm vitest run --maxWorkers=2 packages/runner/test/niche-*.test.ts tools/test/niche-*.test.ts apps/lab/test/niche-*.test.ts
   130	node tools/niche/niche.ts --rung fe --bench                 # step rate, informational (pace if load > 40)
   131	node tools/niche/niche.ts --rung fe --calibrate             # dev + wall + dose ladder only; writes calibration.json
   132	node tools/niche/niche.ts --rung fe --freeze                # lock written; then the freeze commit and push (section 9)
   133	node tools/niche/niche.ts --rung fe --write                 # once; ALWAYS seals a payload (stopped payload if no candidate qualifies; fresh seeds simulated only for qualifying candidates)
   134	node tools/niche/niche.ts --rung fe --check
   135	node tools/niche/niche.ts --rung fe --replay-check
   136	node tools/niche/niche.ts --check && node tools/niche/niche.ts --replay-check   # rung 1 still byte-identical (G7)
   137	pnpm vitest run --maxWorkers=2 packages/sim-ref/test/golden-hashes.test.ts
   138	git diff --exit-code origin/main -- packages/schema packages/sim-ref packages/sim-gpu vendor experiments/preregistration.md experiments/FROZEN docs/rules.md
   139	git diff --name-only origin/main -- docs/niche-construction-2026-11-01   # must list only manifest.json
   140	```
   141	Gates: G1 comparator and ledger tests green, exact zero residual per arm and replicate; G2 fixtures behave as stated (far-field-energy fools `D_inoc` and not `D_inoc2`); G3 per candidate, ordinary-placement dev positive control passes at a dose within its ceiling, else that candidate stops with the minimum detectable dose and no fresh run; G-NOISE per candidate `delta_c` at most half the limiting-contrast control effect; G4 freeze (hash, seeds, amendment, source hashes) committed and pushed before any fresh seed is read, enforced in code (section 9); G5 `--check` and `--replay-check` pass; G6 regeneration budget (full workload, benchmarked with `--bench` before freeze; 3.7 ms/step unloaded): per block `2*T1 + 13 arms * R * T2` = 4,280 steps at R = 4 (about 16 s). Dev candidates 8 blocks x 3 = 24 blocks (about 6.5 min); replicate-noise ensemble about 1 min; wall ladder with stop-at-first-pass, 2 walls x up to 3 doses (60, 120, 240 as the ceiling needs) x 8 seeds x 12 arms x R x 80 = about 184,000 steps worst case (about 11 min; fewer on early passes); fixtures about 1 min; fresh 16 x 3 = 48 blocks (about 13 min). Calibration is run once and reused: `--write` loads the frozen `calibration.json` (verified against the lock) and does not re-derive it. Sum about 35-40 min unloaded, about 80 min at 2x load, plus replay-check (about 13 min); target under 2 hours including reviews, pace if load > 40 (it is above 60 at plan time), and cut scope in this order if the benchmark overruns: page extras, fixture variants, W1 wall; never the comparators, the control or the freeze; G7 rung-1 evidence, calibration and lock byte-identical; only its manifest re-pinned; rung-1 `--check` and `--replay-check` pass; G8 page screenshot spec passes at both widths and the PNGs are read.
   142	
   143	## 9. Freeze-before-run sequence (IMPLEMENT stage)
   144	
   145	1. Implement, unit tests green, dev `--calibrate`; fix the dev-set free parameters (`delta_c`, ceilings and dose rungs, R).
   146	2. Write the dated amendment (section 10, filled with the frozen numbers), `--freeze` to write `protocol.lock.json`, Codex Sol 6.1 review of the freeze (design, seeds, rules), address findings.
   147	3. Commit only the freeze set (code, PLAN, amendment, registry, `calibration.json`, `protocol.lock.json`; no `evidence.json`, `manifest.json`, `EVIDENCE.md`, page data), push `claude/i06-free-energy-matched`, no PR.
   148	4. **Enforced guard.** The rung-fe lock binds protocol, calibration, enumerated seeds, registry digest **and the hashes of every niche source file**. `--write` and `--replay-check` (every path that can simulate fresh seeds) first verify: the lock verifies against current protocol, calibration and sources; the frozen files in the working tree are clean against HEAD; and `git ls-tree origin/claude/i06-free-energy-matched` shows the same lock and frozen sources (pushed freeze). Otherwise they refuse before reading any fresh seed. A guard test stubs git for refuse and allow cases. The lock cannot name its own commit; the pushed-identical check is the ordering proof.
   149	5. Run `--write` (fresh seeds; seals a stopped payload instead if no candidate qualifies), `--check`, `--replay-check`, page and screenshots, write `EVIDENCE.md` from the evidence. Later fixes never edit the frozen rules; changes after the fresh read are recorded as a further amendment and the fresh seeds are then spent.
   150	
   151	## 10. Amendment 3 (dated 2026-11-02; written at freeze, before any fresh seed was read)
   152	
   153	Rung-1 amendments 1 and 2 are the history this builds on (`docs/niche-construction-2026-11-01/PLAN.md` section 10). Dev calibration was run once (`calibration.json`, log `calibrate.log`) on dev seeds 8601001-008, replicate-noise 8601201-204 and wall seeds 8601301-308. What it fixed, and what changed relative to sections 4 and 5:
   154	
   155	- **R = 4**, replicate stride 1000. The replicate-noise control compares replicate set A (offsets 0-3) with a disjoint set B (offsets 4-7) for the builder-removed and the nonbuilder-removed arm, so R is capped at 4 inside the reserved offsets; R = 8 would need a wider registry reservation first. The one allowed escalation to R = 8 was not taken: the wall effects on dev (below) are 10-40 times smaller than the measured floor and zero on most contrasts, so a factor of about sqrt(2) in noise cannot close them.
   156	- **delta per candidate (summed-R units)**: cluster 0: 1152, cluster 4: 1031, cluster 9: 1169 (`max(ceil(B_r * R / 50), ceil(1.5 * max|D|))` over 8 replicate pairs per candidate). Rung 1 had 341 / 291 / 404 for a single sim; divided by R the new floors are 288 / 258 / 292, so the sum of four sims lowered the floor (relative to a single sim) by only 15.5%, 11.4% and 27.7%, not enough.
   157	- **Realistic ceilings** (2 x the median dev builder polymer at T1, the twice-median integer): cluster 0: 200, cluster 4: 204, cluster 9: 169. The ladder 60, 120, 240, 600, 1200 therefore admits 60 and 120 for every candidate, not 240.
   158	- **W2 (polymer floor under the receiver) is unavailable by construction**: ordinary placement requires empty cells and W2 puts polymer on every footprint cell (0 common placeable cells; the probe is part of the evidence). W1 (ring around the footprint) is the only ordinary-placement geometry and is the one control.
   159	- **W1 on dev (8 wall seeds, four gating contrasts including inoc2)**: no dose on the ladder passes. Seeds positive per contrast: dose 60: 4/8 on builder, poly, inoc2; 4/8 on space; dose 120: 4/8, 4/8, 6/8 (space median +27.5), 4/8; dose 240: 5/8, 5/8, 7/8 (space median +29.5), 5/8; dose 600 and 1200 are worse than the comparators (medians about -172 and -190). So the minimum detectable dose does not exist on this ladder (up to 1200, about 6 times the ceiling), the noise gate cannot be met either (it needs the weakest contrast median to be at least twice delta, i.e. above about 2,060 to 2,340; the best weakest-contrast median observed is 1.5), and **all three candidates are `unsupported-measurement` with the stated reason that no ordinary-placement wall is detectable at any tested dose**. (The best weakest-contrast median over the whole ladder is +1.5, at dose 240 and above every ceiling; the best single contrast, space, reaches +29.5 there.) No fresh block is simulated; `--write` seals a stopped payload (fixtures and the frozen calibration only).
   160	- Seeds: block, replicate (primary and noise offsets), wall and fixture seeds are enumerated in the protocol (the lock lists every seed that can run: 8,601,001-8,608,999 only); never 8600101-108 or 8600501-508. Fresh seeds 8601501-516 are listed in the protocol and never read, because no candidate qualified.
   161	- Registry binding: this rung digests its own single reservation line (owner `i06b free-energy-matched`); the first rung's `LATER_SLICE_OWNERS` skips it, so neither rung's evidence depends on the other's reservation. The first rung's manifest.json is re-pinned (source hashes only).
   162	- Dev contrasts for the record: `inoc2` was negative in 24 of 24 dev blocks and equalled `inoc` in all 24, because its maximum retains the first rung's d1E and d1B (`inoc2 <= inoc` by construction), so this is not evidence about the matching itself. The matched-only contrast `inocMatched = Y(b) - max(fU, fS, fB)` (reported, never gating; added at freeze after the Codex freeze review) was zero or negative in 21 of 24 dev blocks and positive in 3. The free-energy gap is real (receiver-alone holds about 19,900 free E against the field arm's about 9,400 at equal total) and is closed by fU/fS/fB exactly.
   163	
   164	Fresh seeds are read only after this amendment, the lock, the calibration and the sources are committed and pushed (`freezeGuard`); with no qualifying candidate the fresh seeds are never read at all.
   165	
   166	Corrections made at freeze after the Codex Sol 6.1 xhigh freeze review (`freeze-review-raw.txt`, `REVIEW.md`): the guard asks the remote (`git ls-remote`) instead of trusting a local tracking ref and lives inside the generator itself; PLAN.md joins the lock and the pushed-identical set; the power statement no longer claims an exclusion bound (a negative is a failure to detect, and the Holm threshold is 13 of 16 with three candidates, 12 with one); the ladder stop rule continues past a dose that passes the wall rule but fails G-NOISE while a within-ceiling dose could still qualify (calibration re-run: byte-identical apart from the new reported `inocMatched` field); fB is described relative to fS (it holds `(eB - eA) * dB` more total energy than fS) with its gap to b recorded with its sign; the numbers above were recomputed from `calibration.json`.
   167	
   168	## 11. Non-goals and limits
   169	
   170	- Not a new organism, predation, or mutation test; one receiver genome, one field per candidate, T1 = 60, T2 = 80, 24x24, single tile.
   171	- Free energy cannot be matched together with total energy at equal matter; reporting both families and the signed `constructionGap` is the honest alternative. A pass shows no more than the power statement in 4.2 allows.
   172	- The positive control is a hand-built wall under ordinary placement; it shows the assay can detect a polymer structure of realistic dose, not that a builder's field can pass. The builder founder-biomass dose-response and a polymer-bearing full-gate trap fixture are cut from this rung and left to a later one. A failed control is a finding about assay sensitivity, not about niche construction.
   173	- Sum-of-R readout reduces replicate noise, not block-to-block field variance; the block count carries that.
   174	- No GPU evidence, no deploy, no edits to frozen preregistration, rung-1 evidence stays sealed and unedited (rung 1's own conclusion is superseded only by a rung-2 label, not rewritten).
   175	- Not part of the frozen preregistration; exploratory.
   176	
   177	## 12. How to try it
   178	
   179	```bash
   180	export PATH=~/.nvm/versions/node/v24.2.0/bin:$PATH
   181	cd /Users/nicholas/develop/browser-life-i06-free-energy-matched && pnpm install
   182	pnpm dev -- --port 5341 --strictPort
   183	# open http://localhost:5341/niche/  (scroll to "Second rung: free-energy-matched")
   184	node tools/niche/niche.ts --rung fe --check          # verifies the sealed second-rung evidence without simulating
   185	node tools/niche/niche.ts --rung fe --replay-check   # regenerates and compares bytes
   186	BL_E2E_PORT=5351 npx playwright test tests/e2e/niche-fe.spec.ts --project=chrome   # screenshots under test-results/
   187	```
   188	What a person sees: per comparator the free energy and total energy before and after matching, the noise floor next to the effect it must detect, the dose ladder of the positive control (pass or the minimum detectable dose), each candidate's edge as supported, unsupported or unavailable, and the `far-field-energy` panel showing the naive metric and the old comparators fooled while the new ones are not.

# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## What this is

An open-ended artificial-life ecology on WebGPU: closed in matter, open in energy, exact integer physics. Individuals, reproduction and heredity are *inferred* from local rules, never declared (no `Organism` class, no `reproduce()`). Research goal and milestones (M3 bootstrap → M4/M5 ensembles): `docs/plan.md`. Rules in prose plus the arithmetic-bounds argument: `docs/rules.md`.

The repo is a pnpm workspace (TypeScript packages + Vite lab), a Deno toolchain for headless GPU runs, and a Phoenix app (`apps/coordinator`). It is jj-colocated with git (`.jj/`).

## Commands

```bash
pnpm install
pnpm dev                 # lab at http://localhost:5173 (/selftest.html, /island.html)
bin/dev                  # lab + coordinator together (PORT, LAB_PORT override 4000/5173)
pnpm typecheck           # tsc over packages/*/src, apps/lab/src, tests (excludes tests/deno)
pnpm test                # vitest: apps/lab/test, packages/*/test, experiments/test, tools/test
pnpm vitest run packages/sim-ref/test/ref.test.ts -t "<name>"   # single test
deno run -A tests/deno/gpu_golden.ts    # GPU vs CPU reference, bit for bit (native WebGPU)
npx playwright test      # browser golden (tests/e2e), Chrome with WebGPU flags; BL_ALL_ENGINES=1 adds firefox/webkit
BL_E2E_PORT=5241 npx playwright test   # a second worktree on its own port (default 5174; strictPort, never reuses a server)
cd apps/coordinator && mix test         # or `mix test test/coordinator/queue_test.exs`
cd apps/coordinator && mix precommit    # warnings-as-errors, format, test
pnpm gen:prereg          # regenerate experiments/preregistration.md generated sections
pnpm gen:cards           # redraw the link-preview cards (site + one per world) with Chrome/WebGPU; then look at them
```

Run `pnpm install` before any `deno run` in a fresh checkout (deno 2.9 otherwise writes `workspaces` into package.json). Tests that start deno pass `--no-lock` (`tools/test/deno-no-lock.test.ts` checks; a launcher or coordinator a test starts that runs deno itself gets the PATH shim in `tests/deno/lib/no_lock_deno.ts`), but a top-level `deno run`, or a tool that starts deno, still writes a stale deno.lock: when package.json deps change, update it deliberately (`deno cache --frozen tools/stitch.ts` fails while it is stale).

`tests/deno/*.ts` are standalone scripts (not `Deno.test`); run each with `deno run -A tests/deno/<name>.ts`. Several need a real GPU; `coordinator_integration.ts` spawns `mix phx.server` against a scratch data dir.

Experiment CLIs (`tools/`, all Deno, native WebGPU): `run.ts` (write run bundles to `runs/<experiment>/<preset>/<condition>/seed-<n>/`), `analyze.ts` (ensemble analysis), `bootstrap.ts` (M3 MAP-Elites search), `retest.ts`, `calibrate.ts`, `island.ts` (headless island), `stitch.ts` (coordinator segments → bundles). Usage is in each file's header comment and in README.md. `runs/` is gitignored and can hold very large outputs.

## Architecture

**Package imports.** `@bl/*` resolve to `packages/*/src/index.ts` via four separate maps that must stay in sync: `tsconfig.json` paths, `vitest.config.ts` aliases, `deno.json` imports, and `apps/lab/vite.config.ts` aliases. There is no build step for packages.

**Two implementations of one rule set.**
- `packages/sim-ref/src/step.ts` is the executable specification (CPU).
- `packages/sim-gpu/src/shaders.ts` (WGSL) must match it bit for bit. `packages/sim-gpu/src/golden.ts` defines golden cases and runs on any WebGPU host (Deno, browser `/selftest.html`, Playwright).
- `packages/sim-ref/test/golden-hashes.test.ts` pins state hashes per `RULE_VERSION` (in `packages/schema/src/config.ts`). Any change to dynamics changes the hashes: an intended rule change bumps `RULE_VERSION` and re-pins deliberately. A change that isn't meant to alter dynamics must leave every pin untouched — new optional `WorldConfig` keys stay absent from `defaultConfig()` so existing configs hash identically.
- All arithmetic is integer (u32 matter, fixed-point controllers, counter-based PRNG keyed on seed/step/cell, stochastic rounding with remainders to the source). Conservation residual is exactly zero. Keep new arithmetic inside the bounds listed in `docs/rules.md` (`MATTER_MAX`, `POOL_MAX`, `mulShr` limits); `validateConfig`/`validateState` enforce them.

**Packages.** `schema` (config, presets, layout, genome, worlds, checkpoint encoding, migration/exchange, M3 founder set) → `sim-ref`, `sim-gpu` → `metrics` (census, tracker, collectives, activity, complexity, stats; runs on readbacks) → `runner` (conditions, `runExperiment`, observers, stitching, island client) and `search` (MAP-Elites, gates). `lineage` (schema, sim-ref) is the lineage inspector's genotype core: keys, ancestry, mutation replay, the controller probe, and the mutation-edge store the lab keeps. `runner` code runs unchanged in browsers and Deno.

**Checkpoints and replay.** A checkpoint artifact carries physics state and observer state together, under one digest. Segmented runs must equal a continuous run byte for byte (`tests/deno/segments.ts`, `tests/deno/stitch.ts`); this determinism is what makes distributed verification possible.

**Archipelago.** `apps/coordinator` (Phoenix, JSON API under `/api`, static status page `priv/static/index.html`) hands islands segment tasks. `Coordinator.Queue` does scheduling, `Coordinator.Segment` owns segment state transitions, and `Coordinator.Store` is content-addressed storage for checkpoints (`.blck`) and bundle files. A fraction of segments is re-run by a second island and compared by state hash. Islands are `apps/lab/island.html` (browser) or `tools/island.ts` (Deno), both via `packages/runner/src/island.ts`. `apps/coordinator/AGENTS.md` has Phoenix/Elixir/OTP conventions to follow there; everything between its `usage-rules` markers is generated by `mix usage_rules.sync` (config in `mix.exs`), so rerun that after dependency changes rather than editing the section. `mix usage_rules.search_docs "<term>" -p <pkg>` searches hexdocs for the coordinator's deps.

**Lab.** `apps/lab/src/sim.worker.ts` owns the WebGPU device, frame loop, rendering and OPFS storage. `apps/lab/src/execution.ts` owns census cadence, observer integrity, checkpoint settlement and replay traversal. State-changing messages run through `exclusive()`, which waits for in-flight frame execution; generation identity invalidates stale readbacks.

**Pre-registration.** `experiments/endpoints.ts` is the source of truth for primary endpoints, held-out observables and activity thresholds, and `tools/analyze.ts` executes it. The generated sections of `experiments/preregistration.md` (between `GENERATED` markers) come from `pnpm gen:prereg`, and `experiments/test/prereg-sync.test.ts` fails when they are stale. Edit `endpoints.ts` and regenerate; never hand-edit inside the markers. The doc is frozen (2026-09-27): its SHA-256 is recorded in `experiments/FROZEN`, `pnpm gen:prereg` now refuses to rewrite the generated sections, and every change goes in a dated amendment section.

**Analysis scale.** Run bundles can be hundreds of MB per run (for example `lineages.tsv`). Analysis tools stream per census rather than loading whole tables; keep new analysis code streaming.

## Codex reviews

Reviews by Codex use **Sol 6.1 at High effort**, not Astra (standing instruction from 2026-10-03, replaces the earlier `gpt-6-astra` policy). Run them read-only at phase boundaries and always before committing:

```bash
codex exec -m gpt-6.1-sol -c model_reasoning_effort=high -s read-only -C <checkout> - < prompt.md > review.txt
```

Use `< /dev/null` when the prompt is an argument (otherwise codex waits on stdin) and `-o <file>` for the final message. Historical "Astra review" comments in the code are provenance for past reviews; leave them. Delegated agents and workspaces follow the same rule.

# I06b Free-energy-matched comparators: niche construction, second rung (slice plan v2, after Codex plan review)

Status: PLAN stage, 2026-11-02. Branch `claude/i06-free-energy-matched` from `origin/main` 0bdd7a70. Sources: "I06" in `docs/simulation-research-2026-11-01/report.md`; the first rung `docs/niche-construction-2026-11-01/` (PLAN, EVIDENCE, REVIEW, sealed `evidence.json`); coordinator decisions D1, D3 in `/Users/nicholas/develop/browser-life-run-1102-log/docs/backlog-run-2026-11-02.html`. Exploratory, CPU only (`sim-ref`; GPU is allowed by the scope but not needed), mutation off, no physics change, no model calls, no deploy. v2 revisions answer `REVIEW.md` "Plan review dispositions" (P1-1..5, P2-6..14). This is a rung on the same tool, not a fork: every new behaviour is keyed off a second protocol object so the first rung's sealed evidence still verifies byte for byte.

## 1. Bounded question

Rung 1 ended `unsupported-measurement` for all three builder candidates (clusters 0, 4, 9). Three defects, each named in `EVIDENCE.md`:

1. **Comparator confound.** Receiver-alone controls (d0, d1E, d1B) match *total* energy but not *free* energy E (cluster 0, seed 8600501: 9,917 vs 22,169, both totals 26,969). The negative inoculum contrasts (23 of 24 blocks) therefore cannot be blamed on inoculum alone.
2. **Noise floor above the effect.** Dev replicate floors delta = 341 / 291 / 404 against builder polymer of 103.5 / 98 / 84.5 and a receiver reserve of B_r = 480.
3. **No positive control at realistic scale.** The hand wall was detected only at 1200 polymer (about 10x what builders leave); at 120 it was not (W1 4/8, W2 3/8 on dev).

Question for this rung: when the receiver-alone benchmark is matched on free energy as well as on total energy and matter, and the readout is powered to a noise floor below the effect it must detect, (a) does the assay pass a positive control at a stated, realistic polymer dose, and, only if so, (b) does a builder's conditioned field causally raise a receiver's receiver-labelled biomass inventory (survival is a viability requirement on the field arm, not a claimed benefit; a paired survival contrast is reported as attribution only) relative to nonbuilder, ablation, scramble and the free-energy-matched receiver-alone controls?

Valid outcomes, all reported as such: `supported` for a pair; `unsupported-within-tested-domain` (a usable negative, only when the positive control passed); `unsupported-measurement` with a stated minimum detectable polymer dose (the "stated reason none exists" branch of the scope); `invalid-or-incomplete`. A negative here does not say a second link is impossible, does not test mutation reaching builder or receiver, and does not test A+B->C.

## 2. What is built (one PR)

1. **Evidence fixtures with a negative case that fools the old inoculum contrast** (section 4.5). New fixture `far-field-energy`: a conditioned field that differs from its controls only by extra free energy laid in a band outside the inoculum footprint plus halo (no polymer). Because ablation only converts P to C (`transfer.ts`), with no polymer `b` and `ab` stay identical and `D_poly = 0`, so the complete rung-1 gate and the new gate both reject it. What the fixture tests is the **inoculum contrast alone**: rung-1 `D_inoc` (vs d0/d1E/d1B/region-matched) reads positive (fooled) while `D_inoc2` (with fU/fS/fB) does not. Evidence records `fullGateLabel` (unsupported, by `D_poly`) and `inocFooledOld` / `inocCaughtNew` separately; the claim made is about the contrast, never "the old rule would label it supported". A full-gate trap needs a polymer-bearing confound and is deferred (section 11). If the fixture cannot fool `D_inoc` at unit scale, the test fails and the slice reports that instead of weakening the claim.
2. **Small CPU path, same library.** Extensions in `packages/runner/src/niche/`: second protocol (`PROTOCOL_FE`), free-energy-matched comparators fU, fS, fB, R-replicate Stage-2 readout, dose-ladder positive control, decision with `inoc2`. `tools/niche/niche.ts` gains `--rung fe` (selects the second protocol and docs directory) in front of every existing mode; no new CLI.
3. **Manifest and machine-readable evidence:** `docs/niche-construction-fe-2026-11-02/{evidence.json,manifest.json,calibration.json,protocol.lock.json}`, sealed like rung 1 with `--write`, `--check`, `--replay-check`.
4. **Local page.** `apps/lab/niche/index.html` gains a "Second rung: free-energy-matched" section (same private route, still 404 on the deployed site) reading the second `evidence.json`: free-energy table per comparator (the 9,917 vs 22,169 style numbers, before and after matching), noise floor vs effect bar, dose-ladder positive-control chart, edge per candidate, the `far-field-energy` panel. Reads evidence only, runs no simulation.
5. Docs: this PLAN (carries the dated amendment, section 10), `REVIEW.md` (Codex dispositions), `EVIDENCE.md` (human result, written after the run).

## 3. Reuse and honest limits of reuse

- Reuse as is: `transfer.ts` (ledger, relabel, placement, scramble), `arms.ts` builders, `assay.ts` stage runner and readouts, `decision.ts` sign test / intersection-union / Holm, `evidence.ts` sealing, the rung-1 page helpers (`render.ts`), `EVIDENCE_STATUSES`. Rung-1 `PROTOCOL` object and every rung-1 code path stay byte-identical in behaviour; `--check` and `--replay-check` for rung 1 are acceptance gates (G7).
- **Registry binding.** Rung 1 hashes the whole registry (minus owners listed in `LATER_SLICE_OWNERS`). This rung adds the owner prefix `i06b free-energy-matched` to that list (the I08 precedent) and binds its own digest over its own reservation only, so neither rung's evidence is invalidated by the other's reservation.
- **Audit.** `transfer.ts` `Audit`/`residuals` have no credit term and `runArm` audits every census. This rung adds **no credit to the audit**: each arm's ledger baseline is that arm's own constructed starting total (lattice + reserve, heatAnalytic 0), so each arm's physical residual is exactly `0n` as in rung 1. The construction difference between `b` and a comparator is a comparison-budget `constructionGap` recorded in evidence only, not a ledger entry (it is not energy created inside any run). If the existing audit cannot take a per-arm baseline, add an optional `baseline` parameter defaulting to current behaviour and prove rung-1 serialization unchanged by test.
- **Manifest conflict (G7).** `tools/niche/niche.ts` `sourceFiles` hashes every niche `.ts` and `checkSaved` rejects changed or added files, so editing existing niche sources or adding modules fails rung-1 `--check` unless the rung-1 `manifest.json` is re-pinned. As in the I08 precedent (rung-1 REVIEW dated note 2026-11-02), a **manifest-only re-pin** of `docs/niche-construction-2026-11-01/manifest.json` is allowed; `evidence.json`, `calibration.json`, `protocol.lock.json`, PLAN, EVIDENCE and REVIEW of rung 1 stay byte-identical to `origin/main`, and rung-1 `--check` and `--replay-check` must pass after the re-pin. New rung-fe code goes in new files wherever possible to keep the diff to existing sources small.
- No change to `packages/schema`, `packages/sim-ref`, `packages/sim-gpu`, `defaultConfig()`, `RULE_VERSION`, golden pins, vendor, preregistration.
- All new arithmetic is analytical, integer (BigInt where it touches totals), outside the step function.

## 4. Design

Everything in rung-1 PLAN section 4 holds unless stated: single 24x24 tile, kernelRadius 5, mutation off, ladder genes, recurring injury with shell, T1 = 60, T2 = 80, reserve R = (480, 960), footprint (14,12) r3, halo 2, absolute clock T1 for every Stage-2 arm, candidates by panel `{0, 4, 9}` and receiver cluster 12, drop order 9 then 4 by runtime only.

### 4.1 Free-energy-matched comparators (answers defect 1)

Free energy means lattice channel E (`sum E`), reported next to total energy (`totalsOf`), for the builder-removed arm `b` and each comparator, at the start of Stage 2 (before inoculum placement) and after placement. Matter is identical in every arm (lattice plus reserve), as in rung 1. Observed rung-1 direction (seed 8600501, cluster 0): the receiver-alone control d0 held MORE free E than `b` (22,169 vs 9,917) with equal total (26,969), because `b` holds its energy as bound matter.

Matching free energy and total energy simultaneously is impossible at equal matter when the comparator has no conditioned composition (bound matter carries energy at eB, eP, eC, eA). Each comparator is a separately constructed initial state (not a transformation of `b` inside a run); its own ledger starts from its own total and conserves exactly. The difference is reported as `constructionGap = totalEnergy(b) - totalEnergy(f)` (signed BigInt, evidence only, never in the ledger): **gap > 0** means the comparator holds less total energy than the field arm; **gap < 0** means it holds more (a stronger comparator, the conservative side for the claim).

- **fU (uniform free-matched):** receiver-alone lattice, uniform A, free E set uniform to `sum E(b)` over the lattice.
- **fS (spatial free-matched):** as fU but E copied cell for cell from `b` (same free-energy field, no polymer, no biomass, matter all A in the remainder). Isolates "the energy pattern alone" from "structure".
- **fB (free-matched plus biomass-matched):** fS plus the rung-1 d1B raise of B (region plus halo, matter converted from A), **without** the `(eB - eA)*dB` lattice debit, so free E stays equal to `b` cell for cell before and after placement; fB holds `(eB - eA) * dB` more total energy than fS (the bound-energy increase is extra total energy, never a ledger entry). Its own `constructionGap` to `b` is recorded with its sign (positive when the field binds more energy in polymer or waste than fB is handed, negative or zero otherwise; `comparator-excess` where it is negative). Test: free-E equality before and after placement, and the fS-to-fB gap difference.
- Rung-1 d0, d1E, d1B are kept unchanged and reported (total-matched family).

New inoculum contrast: `D_inoc2 = Y(b | builder) - max(Y(d1E), Y(d1B), Y(fU), Y(fS), Y(fB))`; every constructed comparator is in the max (none excluded by sign of gap; `comparator-excess` and `comparator-deficit` are labels in evidence). Rung-1 `D_inoc` is still computed and reported next to it. The four gating contrasts are builder, poly, space, inoc2. A comparator whose construction would need negative lattice E or exceed `POOL_MAX`/`MATTER_MAX` is refused (`credit-overdraw`, never clamped); a block where fU, fS and fB are all refused is `invalid-or-incomplete` for that candidate (reported, never dropped).

### 4.2 Better-powered readout (answers defect 2)

- **Replicated Stage 2.** Each arm's Stage 2 is run for `R = 4` sim seeds (block seed plus fixed offsets `{0, 1, 2, 3} * 1000`), identical offsets in every arm (so injury steps and RNG keys still coincide across arms); Stage 1 histories and transfers are shared. Primary endpoint `Y = sum over the R replicates of receiver-labelled B at T2` (integer; no division, units are x R of rung 1). Viability: receiver B above zero in at least `R - 1` of R replicates counts the field arm `b` as viable for that block. Viability is a requirement for the gate (field arm viable in at least 14 of 16 blocks; at a true per-block rate of 0.9, 15/16 would pass only 51% of the time). A paired survival contrast (arm vs comparator) is reported as attribution, never gating, and no survival benefit is claimed.
- **Replicate seeds** are enumerated and reserved explicitly (5.1) for the maximum R of 8.
- **More blocks:** 16 fresh blocks per candidate (was 8). Exact sign test over 16; `k = 16` gives 1/65,536, `k = 14` gives 137/65,536, `k = 12` gives 2,517/65,536 (hand-enumerated in tests). Candidate threshold by Holm at alpha 0.05 over 3 candidates as before (1/60, 1/40, 1/20 for the three ordered p-values).
- **Power statement (frozen wording, corrected at freeze after the Codex freeze review; see section 10).** With three candidates running the first Holm rejection needs k >= 13 of 16 (p = 697/65,536; 12 if only one candidate runs). A contrast whose true per-block positive probability is 0.75 passes that single threshold 40.5% of the time, 0.85 gives 79%, 0.90 gives 93%, 0.95 gives 99%; four contrasts, delta and viability can only lower the pass rate. A negative is a failure to detect a consistent benefit under these rules, not an exclusion bound on the effect size and not evidence of no benefit. Dev reports the empirical positive-block rate of the wall control at the qualifying dose next to this table.
- **Delta from a replicate control with the same readout:** `delta = max(ceil(0.02 * B_r * R), 1.5 * max|D|)` over dev replicate pairs (nonbuilder vs nonbuilder, builder vs builder, on a disjoint replicate seed set of R sims). With the sum over R the expected floor falls by about sqrt(R) relative to rung 1 after rescaling; dev says whether it did.
- Secondary readout (reported, never gating): per-replicate area under the receiver-B census curve (sum over the 8 censuses), as a lower-variance cross-check.
- **Noise gate (G-NOISE, dev only, before any fresh seed), per candidate:** `delta_c` must be at most half of the wall control's median effect on the **limiting gating contrast** (the one with the smallest median effect among the four), in summed-R units, at that candidate's qualifying dose (4.3). Otherwise the rung stops `unsupported-measurement` and reports the achieved floor and the minimum detectable dose; the fresh seeds are not read.

### 4.3 Positive control at realistic scale (answers defect 3)

The hand-wall fixtures (W1 ring, W2 floor under the receiver; rung-1 definitions) run the full four-contrast gate on dev wall seeds across a dose ladder of total polymer `P in {60, 120, 240, 600, 1200}`, ascending, stopping each wall at its first passing dose (that dose is its minimum detectable dose); doses above the ceiling are run only if nothing at or below it passes, to state the minimum detectable dose.

- **Per-candidate realistic ceiling** `C_c = 2 x` the median dev builder polymer at T1 of candidate c (rung 1 measured 84.5-103.5, so C is about 170-207, which admits 60 and 120, not 240). The control for candidate c uses doses `<= C_c` only.
- **Ordinary placement required.** The rung-1 `wallGateBlock` uses `allowP: true` (overwriting polymer on injured cells), which real transfers (`placeAll` default false) forbid. The qualifying control must place the wall by the ordinary placement rule (no overwrite of existing polymer, same pool-cap and reserve guards) at cells where that is legal; wall variants that need `allowP: true` stay as a reported diagnostic and never qualify the assay. If no ordinary-placement wall geometry exists, the control is `unavailable` and the stated reason is that none exists, ending the rung `unsupported-measurement`.
- **Wall pass rule (dev, 8 wall seeds):** for each of the four gating contrasts, at least 7 of 8 seeds positive (exact p = 9/256) and median >= `delta_c`. A wall passes at dose P iff all four hold. The control **qualifies candidate c** iff W1 or W2 passes at some dose `<= C_c` and G-NOISE holds (4.2).
- A candidate that does not qualify ends `unsupported-measurement` with the minimum detectable dose, its ratio to the builder polymer and the floor that explains it; its fresh blocks are not simulated (a sealed stopped payload is still written, section 8). Candidates qualify independently.

Dropped from this rung (cut for scope, section 11): the builder founder-biomass dose-response knob.

### 4.4 Readouts, ledger and flux (unchanged from rung 1, per replicate)

Per arm and replicate: receiver survival, receiver-labelled biomass inventory, flux attribution (synthesis share; "growth" only at share at least half and no saturated counter), builder cost, before/after inventories. Ledger invariant per arm: lattice + reserve + cumulative heatAnalytic against that arm's own starting total, exact `0n` at every census of both stages, including every replicate (the unchanged rung-1 audit; no credit term). Evidence adds per arm: free E, total energy, `constructionGap` vs `b`.

### 4.5 Fixtures

16x16, kernelRadius 5 at unit scale (rung-1 constraint); the positive control is additionally qualified at the frozen scale on dev.
- `far-field-energy` (new, 2.1: fools rung-1 `D_inoc`, not `D_inoc2`; full gate rejects via `D_poly = 0`); also a uniform and an in-footprint variant, `caughtBy` recorded.
- `free-gap-replay`: rung-1 observed direction at fixture scale (d0 holds MORE free E than `b` with equal totals; bound inventories as recorded); asserts fU/fS/fB reproduce `b`'s free E exactly (difference zero), that d0's excess is removed, and that `constructionGap` is recorded with every arm's own ledger residual `0n`.
- `takeover-only`, `null-field`, `hand-wall-gate-W2` (rung-1 fixtures re-run with the second protocol; expectations unchanged).
- Guards re-run: overwrite, pool cap, underfunded reserve, exhausted reserve, plus `credit-overdraw` (a free-matched comparator that would need negative lattice E or exceed a cap is refused, not clamped).

## 5. Pre-stated rules (frozen before any fresh-seed run; dated amendment in section 10)

### 5.1 Seeds
Block seeds in `8,601,001-8,601,999` and every derived replicate seed are reserved in `experiments/discovery/seed-registry.json` (owner `i06b free-energy-matched`) **before** anything runs. Replicate sims use block seed + `k * 1000`, `k = 0..7` (R = 4 uses k = 0..3; R = 8 uses k = 0..7), so derived seeds reach 8,608,999: the reservation lists the contiguous range `8,601,001-8,608,999`; the implementer first runs `seedCollisions` over it and, if any existing owner collides, replaces the offset by a collision-free stride and records that in the amendment. The protocol hash binds the explicit enumerated list of every seed that can run (block, replicate, noise, wall, fixture) and the registry digest; `--check` rescans the enumerated list, not only base lists. Never reused: 8,600,101-108, 8,600,501-508, nor any other rung-1 seed. Dev blocks 8,601,001-8,601,008, dev replicate-noise 8,601,201-8,601,204, wall 8,601,301-8,601,308, fixtures 8,601,401-8,601,408, fresh 8,601,501-8,601,516. Dev outcomes may set only `T1, T2` (unchanged unless dev shows cause), `delta_c`, the dose rungs and ceilings, and `R` (4 or 8; one escalation to 8 allowed if the noise gate fails, recorded before the freeze). Fresh seeds are read once, after the freeze commit is pushed and verified (section 9).

### 5.2 Estimands
Primary `Y` as in 4.2, paired within a block. Gating contrasts: `D_builder`, `D_poly`, `D_space`, `D_inoc2`. Attribution only: `D_spaceP`, `D_spaceNP`, rung-1 `D_inoc`, `D_naive = Y(b) - Y(d0)`.

### 5.3 Edge label
Rung-1 rules with these changes only: 16 blocks; `inoc2` replaces `inoc`; per-contrast exact one-sided sign test (zeros non-positive) with `median >= delta`; field arm viable in at least 14 of 16 blocks (viability requirement, no survival-benefit claim); candidate p = max over four; Holm across candidates that ran. Adequacy is per candidate: the ordinary-placement positive control qualified that candidate at a dose within its ceiling and G-NOISE held (4.3); the rung-1 polymer criterion is a reported field only. `unsupported-within-tested-domain` only when valid, adequately measured and a gating test fails; its meaning is bounded by the power statement in 4.2. Mechanism text and the three-valued panel verdict as rung 1.

# I06b evidence: free-energy-matched comparators, second rung (2026-11-02)

Machine-readable: `evidence.json` (sealed, seal 42ba2c6b3e935f32...), `manifest.json`, `calibration.json`, `protocol.lock.json`. Logs: `calibrate.log`, `write.log`, `checks.log`, `rung1-replay.log`. Freeze commit e7a48c06, pushed to `origin/claude/i06-free-energy-matched` before `--write` (the generator refuses otherwise). Exploratory, CPU only, mutation off.

## Result

**unsupported-measurement for all three candidates (clusters 0, 4, 9). Not a negative.** The ordinary-placement positive control found no detectable wall at any tested dose, so by the pre-stated rule no fresh builder block was simulated (fresh seeds 8601501-516 never read). This is the stated reason "none exists": no ordinary-placement wall geometry is detectable at realistic scale in this assay.

- Placement: W2 (polymer floor under the receiver) is unavailable (0 common placeable cells); W1 (ring) is the only legal geometry.
- W1 dose ladder (8 wall seeds, four gating contrasts incl. inoc2, R = 4): 60, 120, 240 never reach 7 of 8 on all contrasts (best weakest-contrast median +1.5 at 240, above every ceiling); 600 and 1200 are worse than the comparators (medians about -172 and -190). Ceilings (2 x builder polymer): 200, 204, 169.
- Noise floor delta (summed R = 4): 1152, 1031, 1169. Divided by R that is 288, 258, 292 against rung 1's 341, 291, 404 (floor lowered 15.5%, 11.4%, 27.7%): not enough, and the wall effect is far below even the new floor (G-NOISE needs the weakest median at least 2 x delta).
- Comparators: fU, fS, fB match the field arm's free energy exactly (9,394 for cluster 0 seed 8601001, against receiver-alone's 19,864 at equal total 25,624) with exact zero ledger residuals; they hold less total energy (constructionGap +10,470, +10,470, +10,380). `inoc2` negative in 24 of 24 dev blocks and equal to `inoc` (its max keeps d1E/d1B); matched-only contrast `inocMatched` was zero or negative in 21 and positive in 3 (reported, not gating).
- far-field-energy fixture (8 seeds): naive 8/8 benefit; rung-1 inoculum contrast 8/8 positive (fooled); free-energy-matched `inoc2` 0/8 (caught); full gate rejects (poly 0/8). The claim is about the inoculum contrast only. Hand-wall W2 (allowP, frozen scale, diagnostic) passes the full gate: supported (7/8 on builder, poly, inoc2; 8/8 space), so the gate can say supported for a floor of 1200 polymer, which ordinary placement forbids.

## What this does and does not say

The assay at this scale cannot see a polymer structure of builder-like dose, so it cannot test whether a builder's field helps a receiver; it says nothing against niche construction. Next rung candidates (not run): a placement-legal control with real effect at small dose (e.g. protective polymer on cells the receiver may occupy via a different transfer rule), or a lower-variance readout than receiver biomass at T2.
